2
Tschirnhaus Complete Intersections

Given a polynomial

p(z)=z^{n}+a_{1}z^{n-1}+\cdots+a_{n}=\prod(z-x_{i}),

a
Tschirnhaus transformation
is a “change of variables”

y=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}x^{j}.

This gives a new polynomial

q(z)=z^{n}+c_{1}z^{n-1}+\cdots+c_{n}=\prod_{i}(z-y_{i}).

We are interested in Tschirnhaus transformations such that
q(z)
is “better normalized” than
p(z)
, e.g. in the sense that for some
i
,

\sum_{j}y_{j}^{i}=0,

or more generally such that

\sum_{j}y_{j}^{i_{1}}=\cdots=\sum_{j}y_{j}^{i_{k}}=0

for some
i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}
. In this section, we study the collection of all
\mathbf{b}=(b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1})
such that the above normalizations hold. These are affine varieties which we denote
\widetilde{T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}}
, and we refer to their projectivizations
T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}
as
Tschirnhaus complete intersections
.

In this section, we introduce the varieties
T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}
as objects of interest in their own right, i.e. via explicit equations. We relate them to classical examples of interest, and study their geometry. In Section
3
, we review the classical subject of Tschirnhaus transformations for algebraic functions, and we identify the varieties
T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}
considered here with the spaces of “normalized changes of variables” described above.

Tschirnhaus Complete Intersections via Explicit Equations

Fix
n\geq 0
. In this section, we work over
\mathbb{Z}
unless otherwise specified, so that, e.g.
\mathbb{A}^{n}:=\Spec(\mathbb{Z}[a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}])
. For ease of reading, we adopt the following notation.

Notation 2.1
.

Denote

\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathbf{a}:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{A}^{n}.&&&&|\kappa|:=\sum_{i}k_{i}\\
\mathbf{b}:=[b_{0}:\cdots:b_{n-1}]\in\mathbb{P}^{n-1}&&&&||\kappa||:=\sum_{i}i\cdot k_{i}\\
\kappa:=(k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}&&&&\mathbf{b}^{\kappa}:=\prod_{i}b_{i}^{k_{i}}\end{array}

For
|\kappa|=i
, recall the multinomial coefficients

\binom{i}{\kappa}:=\binom{i}{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}:=\frac{i!}{k_{0}!\cdots k_{n-1}!}.

We also introduce two variants of the above.

Notation 2.2
.

{}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}

\displaystyle:=[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-1}]\in\mathbb{P}^{n-2}

{}^{\prime}\kappa

\displaystyle:=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1})\in\mathbb{N}^{n-1}

{}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-2}]\in\mathbb{P}^{n-3}

{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-2})\in\mathbb{N}^{n-2}

Mutatis mutandis, we will also write
|^{\prime}\kappa|
,
||^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}||
,
\binom{i}{{}^{\prime}\kappa}
, etc. Note that the meaning of
||-||
depends on whether the first coordinate is the zeroth coordinate or the first coordinate. Our notation indicates that any tuple without a
′
preceding its label starts with a zeroth coordinate, while any tuple with a
′
preceding its label starts with a first coordinate.

We now inductively define polynomials in the
a_{i}
by

\displaystyle p_{0}

\displaystyle:=n,

(2.1)

while, for
0<k\leq n

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle:=ka_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}a_{k-i}p_{i},

(2.2)

and for
k>n

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{i=k-n}^{k-1}a_{k-i}p_{i}.

(2.3)

Remark 2.3
.

To interpret the polynomials
p_{i}
, let
\sigma_{i}
denote the
i^{th}
elementary symmetric polynomial in formal variables
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
. If we write
a_{i}=(-1)^{i}\sigma_{i}
, then Newton’s Identities give

p_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}^{i}.

Definition 2.4
.

For
i,n\geq 1
, let the
T_{i}^{n}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}
be the variety defined by the vanishing of the polynomial

\sum_{\kappa~s.t.~|\kappa|=i}\binom{i}{\kappa}p_{||\kappa||}{\bf b}^{\kappa}.

(2.4)

Note that this polynomial is homogeneous of degree
i
in the
\mathbf{b}
-coordinates. Projecting onto the first factor gives a family of degree
i
hypersurfaces in
\mathbb{P}^{n-1}

T_{i}^{n}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

We refer to this family as the
n^{th}
Tschirnhaus hypersurface of degree
i
. When the superscript
n
is clear from context, we will suppress it for ease of reading.

Definition 2.5
.

Fix
n\geq 1
. For
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}
, define the
n^{th}
Tschirnhaus complete intersection
T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}
(of multi-degree
i_{1}\cdots i_{k}
)
to be the variety defined by the vanishing of the polynomials (
2.4
) for
i=i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}
. Equivalently, define

T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}:=T^{n}_{i_{1}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}}\cdots\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}}T^{n}_{i_{k}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}.

Define the
n^{th}
reduced Tschirnhaus complete intersection
T^{n^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}
(of multi-degree
i_{1}\cdots i_{k}
)
by

T^{n^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}:=T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\cap\{b_{0}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}.

Example 2.6
.

The hyperplane
T_{1}(\mathbf{a})\subset\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}
is given by the equation

nb_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}p_{i}b_{i}=0

Over
\mathbb{Z}[1/n]
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-2}

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.86868pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.49368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.98737pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>T_{1}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a},[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-1}])

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathbf{a},[-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}p_{i}b_{i}:b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-1}]).

Likewise, the hyperplane
T^{\prime}_{1}(\mathbf{a})\subset\mathbb{P}^{n-2}_{\mathbf{b}}
is given by the equation

\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}p_{i}b_{i}=0

Over each locus
\{p_{i}\neq 0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
for
1\leq i<n
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-3}

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.86868pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.49368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.98737pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>T_{1}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a},[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{\hat{i}}:\cdots:b_{n-2}])

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathbf{a},[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{i-1}:\frac{-1}{p_{i}}\sum_{j\neq i}p_{j}b_{j}:b_{i+1}:\cdots b_{n-2}]).

As a warm-up to Theorem
2.12
below, we prove the following.

Lemma 2.7
.

The families of quadrics
T_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
and
T^{\prime}_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
are generically smooth.

Remark 2.8
.

The statement of the lemma for
T_{12}
(and most likely for
T^{\prime}_{12}
) is classical, and follows from the fact that the discriminant of the quadratic form defining
T_{12}(\mathbf{a})
is equal to
\frac{1}{n}
times the discriminant of the polynomial
x^{n}+a_{1}x^{n-1}+\cdots+a_{n}
(see, e.g.
[
Sy1887
, p. 468-469]
). We give a different proof in order to warm-up for Theorem
2.12
.

Proof of Lemma
2.7
.

The quadric
T_{12}(\mathbf{a})\subset\mathbb{P}^{n-2}_{\mathbf{b}}
is given, in coordinates
[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-1}]
by the equation

-\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}p_{i}b_{i}\right)^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n-1}p_{i+j}b_{i}b_{j}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}p_{2i}b_{i}^{2}=0.

We now specialize to the radical pencil
x^{n}+a=0
, i.e.
\mathbf{a}=(0,\ldots,0,a)
. Then
T_{12}(a):=T_{12}(0,\ldots,a)
is given by the equation

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}-2na\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}b_{i}b_{n-i}\right)=0&n~\text{ odd}\\
-na\left(b_{\frac{n}{2}}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}b_{i}b_{n-i}\right)=0&n~\text{ even}\end{array}\right.

The partial derivatives of the defining polynomial of
T_{12}(a)
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T_{12}(a)

\displaystyle=-2nab_{n-j}.

We see that these vanish simultaneously if and only if
b_{j}=0
for all
j
, i.e.
T_{12}(a)
is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}[1/2n]
so long as
a\neq 0
(and thus
T_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
is generically smooth).

We now prove
T^{\prime}_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
is generically smooth. Using (
2.2
), the hyperplane
T^{\prime}_{1}(a)
is given by

(n-1)ab_{n-1}=0.

Over
\mathbb{Z}[1/(n-1)]
, and
a\neq 0
, we can therefore use the coordinates

[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-2}]

on
T_{1}^{\prime}(a)
. In these coordinates, and abusing notation by writing the same symbol for a hypersurface and its defining polynomial, we have

T_{12}^{\prime}(a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}-2(n-1)a\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}b_{i}b_{n-1-i}\right)&n~\text{ even}\\
-(n-1)a(b_{\frac{n-1}{2}}^{2}+\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}b_{i}b_{n-1-i}\right)&n~\text{ odd}\end{array}\right.

The partial derivatives of
T_{12}^{\prime}(a)
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T^{\prime}_{12}(a)

\displaystyle=-2(n-1)ab_{n-1-j}.

We see that these vanish simultaneously if and only if
b_{j}=0
for all
j
, i.e.
T^{\prime}_{12}(a)
is smooth over
\mathbb{Z}[1/2(n-1)]
so long as
a\neq 0
(and thus
T^{\prime}_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
is generically smooth).
∎

Tschirnhaus hypersurfaces as spaces of maps.

In Section
3
, we explain the origin of the Tschirnhaus complete intersections in the classical study of formulas for the general degree
n
polynomial (beginning with
[
Ts1683
]
). For the moment, we just observe that several varieties of classical interest are closely related to
T_{i}^{n}
for small
i,n
.

Let
\mathbf{x}:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
be coordinates on affine
n
-space, denoted
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}
. Let
\sigma_{i}(\mathbf{x})
denote the
i^{th}
elementary symmetric function on the
x_{i}
, and consider the map

\displaystyle q\colon\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\mapsto(-\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{x}),\ldots,(-1)^{n}\sigma_{n}(\mathbf{x})).

By Newton’s Theorem, this map realizes
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
as the quotient of
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}
by the permutation action of the symmetric group
S_{n}
on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}
. As remarked above, Newton’s Identities imply that

p_{i}(q(\mathbf{x}))=\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}^{i}.

Let
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}:=(b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1})
viewed as affine coordinates on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}
. The relative affine cone on the pullback
T_{i}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}
is given by

\widetilde{T_{i}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}}}:=\left\{(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}~|~\sum\sum_{\kappa~s.t.~|\kappa|=i}\binom{i}{\kappa}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}^{||\kappa||}\right){\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}^{\kappa}=0\right\}.

Consider the map

\displaystyle\ev\colon\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}x_{1}^{j},\ldots,\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}x_{n}^{j}).

Lemma 2.9
.

In the notation above,

\widetilde{T_{i}|_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}}}=\ev^{-1}(\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{x}}~|~\sum_{j}x_{j}^{i}=0.\}).

Proof.

We prove this by explicit computation. For
i\geq 0
, write

p_{i}(\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}x_{\ell}^{i}.

In particular,
p_{0}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=n
. Let
\ev(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})_{\ell}:=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}x_{\ell}^{j}
. By the Multinomial Theorem,

\displaystyle p_{i}(\ev(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{b}}))=\sum_{\ell}\ev(\mathbf{x},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})_{\ell}^{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}x_{\ell}^{j}\right)^{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell}\left(\sum_{\kappa~s.t.~|\kappa|=i}\binom{i}{\kappa}{\bf b}^{\kappa}x_{\ell}^{||\kappa||}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\kappa~s.t.|\kappa|=i}\binom{i}{\kappa}p_{||\kappa||}{\bf b}^{\kappa}

(2.7)

where, in the final line, we use Newton’s Identities to identify the power sums with the polynomials
p_{||\kappa||}
in the
a_{i}
defined in Equations
2.1
-
2.3
.

Setting the form (
2.7
) to 0, we obtain the hypersurface
\widetilde{T_{i}^{n}}
as claimed.
∎

Example 2.10
.

Let
S\subset\mathbb{P}^{4}
be the Clebsch diagonal surface, i.e. the complete intersection

S:=\{[x_{1}:\cdots:x_{5}]\in\mathbb{P}^{4}~|~\sum_{i=1}^{5}x_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{5}x_{i}^{3}=0\}.

Let
\widetilde{S}\subset\mathbb{A}^{5}_{\mathbf{x}}
be the affine cone over
S
. Then

\widetilde{T^{5}_{13}|_{\mathbb{A}^{5}_{\mathbf{x}}}}=\ev^{-1}(\widetilde{S}).

As observed by Klein
[
Kl1884
, Part II, Ch. 2]
,
\widetilde{T^{5}_{13}|_{\mathbb{A}^{5}_{\mathbf{x}}}}
can be understood as a space of
S_{5}
-equivariant maps of
\mathbb{A}^{5}_{\mathbf{x}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\widetilde{S}
.

Example 2.11
.

Let
F\subset\mathbb{P}^{6}
be the symmetric Fano sextic 3-fold as in
[
Be12
]
, i.e. the complete intersection

F:=\{[x_{1}:\cdots:x_{7}]\in\mathbb{P}^{6}~|~\sum_{i=1}^{7}x_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{7}x_{i}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{7}x_{i}^{3}=0\}.

Let
\widetilde{F}\subset\mathbb{A}^{7}_{\mathbf{x}}
be the affine cone over
F
. Then

\widetilde{T^{7}_{123}|_{\mathbb{A}^{7}_{\mathbf{x}}}}=\ev^{-1}(\widetilde{F}).

Though not remarked upon in
[
Be12
]
, the symmetric Fano sextic arises as the “root space” of the normal form for the general degree 7 polynomial considered by Hilbert in his 13th problem
[
Hi1900
]
:

z^{7}+az^{3}+bz^{2}+cz+1=0.

.
The variety
\widetilde{T^{7}_{123}|_{\mathbb{A}^{7}_{\mathbf{x}}}}
can be understood as a space of
S_{7}
-equivariant maps of
\mathbb{A}^{7}_{\mathbf{x}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\widetilde{F}
, equivalently of ways of converting the general degree 7 polynomial into Hilbert’s normal form.

Geometry of Tschirnhaus Complete Intersections

We can now state our main geometric theorem.

Theorem 2.12
.

Let
p
be a prime. Let
i=p^{r}+1<n
for some prime power
p^{r}
with
r>0
.

1.

If
p\nmid n
, the family of Tschirnhaus complete intersections

T_{12i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

is generically smooth (i.e. there is a Zariski open
U\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
such that for all
\mathbf{a}\in U
,
T_{12i}(\mathbf{a})
is a smooth complete intersection).

2.

If
p\mid n
, the family of reduced Tschirnhaus complete intersections

T_{12i}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

is generically smooth.

Deferring the proof for a moment, let
K
be a field of characteristic 0, now and throughout this paper.

We now record a special case of Kleiman’s Bertini Theorem
[
Kl74
]
; for ease of reading, we include the proof below.

Proposition 2.13

(Bertini for isotropics)
.

Let
K
be algebraically closed. Let
X
be a
K
-variety. Let
Q\subset\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}
be a smooth family of quadrics over
X
. For
k\leq\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\rfloor
, let
Gr(k,Q)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
denote the relative Grassmannian of
k
-dimensional isotropic subspaces in
Q
, and let
\mathcal{L}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Gr(k,Q)
denote the tautological bundle. Let
Y\subset\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}
be a smooth family of varieties over
X
such that the family
Q\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
is smooth over some dense open
V\subset X
. Then there exists a dense open
U\subset Gr(k,Q)|_{V}
such that the family
\mathcal{L}|_{U}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y|_{V}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
is smooth.

Combining Theorem
2.12
, Lemma
2.7
and Proposition
2.13
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 2.14
.

Let
Gr(T_{12})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
denote the relative Grassmannian of maximal isotropics in the family of quadrics
T_{12}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
, and let
\mathcal{L}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Gr(T_{12})
denote the tautological bundle (with similar notation for the analogous objects for
T^{\prime}_{12}
). Let
p
be a prime and let
i=p^{r}+1
for some
r>0
.

1.

If
p\nmid n
, there exists a dense open
V\subset Gr(T_{12})
such that

\mathcal{L}|_{V}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}}T_{12i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

is smooth (i.e. for the generic polynomial, the intersection of
T_{12i}(\mathbf{a})
with a maximal isotropic in
T_{12}(\mathbf{a})
is smooth).

2.

If
p\mid n
, there exists a dense open
V\subset Gr(T_{12}^{\prime})
such that

\mathcal{L}|_{V}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-2}_{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}}}T_{12i}^{\prime}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

is smooth.

Proof.

Note that to prove the existence of an open dense
V
, it suffices to restrict all of the varieties over
\mathbb{Z}
above to a geometric generic point
\Spec(K)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
. The result now follows immediately from Theorem
2.12
, Lemma
2.7
and Proposition
2.13
.
∎

Remark 2.15
.

Corollary
2.14
(for the case
p=2,i=3,n=9
) fills the gap in Hilbert’s argument remarked upon by Dixmier
[
Di93
,
S
8]
.

Proof of Proposition
2.13
.

We recall Kleiman’s proof
[
Kl74
]
. Consider the canonical map

\displaystyle\pr_{2}\colon\mathcal{L}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Q

(coming from the construction of
\mathcal{L}
as an incidence variety
\mathcal{L}\subset Gr(k,Q)\times_{X}Q
). Observe that this map is smooth: indeed, the relative group scheme
{\rm O}(Q)
acts transitively over
X
on both
\mathcal{L}
and
Q
(i.e. it acts transitively on fibers over
X
) and the map
\mathcal{L}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Q
is an
{\rm O}(Q)
-equivariant fiber bundle, with fiber at
v\in Q
given by
\Stab_{{\rm O}(Q)}(v)/\Stab_{{\rm O}(Q)}(L,v)
, (n.b. the stabilizer of an isotropic point
v
is a maximal parabolic, and the stabilizer of the flag
v\in L
is a sub-parabolic).

Let
V\subset X
be a dense open such that
Q\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
is smooth over
V
. Shrinking
V
as necessary, we can assume without loss of generality that
V
is a smooth variety over
K
(note that we are using characteristic 0 here), and thus
(Q\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}
is also a smooth
K
-variety. Now consider the fiber product

\textstyle{(\mathcal{L}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{(Q\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{\mathcal{L}|_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Q|_{V}}
\textstyle{Gr(k,Q)|_{V}}

The map
f
is smooth because
\pr_{2}
is smooth. Because
(Q\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}
is a smooth
K
-variety, the
K
-variety
(\mathcal{L}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}
is smooth. We therefore have a dominant map of smooth
K
-varieties

q=\pi\circ g\colon(\mathcal{L}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y)|_{V}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Gr(k,Q)|_{V}.

By generic smoothness (e.g.
[
Ha77
, Corollary III.10.7]
), there exists a nonempty open subset
U\subset Gr(k,Q)|_{V}
such that
q\colon(\mathcal{L}|_{U}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y|_{V})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>U
is smooth, and thus the composite
(\mathcal{L}|_{U}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{n}_{X}}Y|_{V})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>U\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>V
is smooth as well.
∎

We now prove Theorem
2.12
.

Proof of Theorem
2.12
.

We prove the two cases separately, via parallel arguments. As in the proof of Lemma
2.7
, if it will not cause confusion, we will abuse notation by writing the same symbol to denote a complete intersection and its defining polynomials.

Case 1:
p\nmid n
.
The complete intersection
T_{12i}({\bf a})
is smooth if and only if the
3\times n
matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\partial_{b_{1}}T_{1}({\bf a})&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-1}}T_{1}({\bf a})\\
\partial_{b_{1}}T_{2}({\bf a})&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-1}}T_{2}({\bf a})\\
\partial_{b_{1}}T_{i}({\bf a})&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-1}}T_{i}({\bf a})\end{array}\right)

has full rank for all
{\bf b}\in T_{12i}({\bf a})
. Choosing coordinates on
T_{1}
, we can equivalently check whether the
2\times(n-1)
matrix given by the partials of
T_{12}
and
T_{1i}
has rank 2 for all
{\bf b}\in T_{12i}({\bf a})
. To show generic smoothness, it suffices to find a single
{\bf a}
for which this holds. Further, because the matrix above is defined over
\mathbb{Z}
, to show it is nonsingular in characteristic 0, it suffices to find a prime
p
for which its reduction mod
p
is nonsingular.

We specialize to the locus of radical polynomials, i.e. those of the form

p(x)=x^{n}+a

i.e.
{\bf a}=(0,\ldots,0,a)
. It suffices to show there exists
a
such that
T_{12i}(a):=T_{12i}(0,\ldots,a)
is smooth. Note that, restricting to
x^{n}+a
, the hyperplane
T_{1}(a)
is given by

nb_{0}=0.

We can therefore use the coordinates

[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-1}]

on
T_{1}(a)
as above. As in (
2
), the form
T_{12}(a)
is given in these coordinates by

T_{12}(a)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}-2na\left(\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}b_{i}b_{n-i}\right)&n~\text{ odd}\\
-na\left(b_{\frac{n}{2}}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}b_{i}b_{n-i}\right)&n~\text{ even}\end{array}\right.

and the partial derivatives are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T_{12}(a)

\displaystyle=-2nab_{n-j}.

Similarly, using Notation
2.2
, the form
T_{1i}(a)
is given by

T_{1i}(a)=n\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-1)^{\ell}a^{\ell}\left(\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa~s.t.|^{\prime}\kappa|=i,||^{\prime}\kappa||=\ell n}\binom{i}{{}^{\prime}\kappa}{\bf{}^{\prime}b}^{{}^{\prime}\kappa}\right)\right)

The partial derivatives of
T_{1i}(a)
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T_{1i}(a)

\displaystyle=in\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-1)^{\ell}a^{\ell}\left(\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa|=i-1,||^{\prime}\kappa||+j=\ell n}\binom{i-1}{{}^{\prime}\kappa}{\bf{}^{\prime}b}^{{}^{\prime}\kappa}\right)\right).

Define

\displaystyle T_{j,12}(a)

\displaystyle:=ab_{n-j}

\displaystyle T_{j,1i}(a)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-1)^{\ell}a^{\ell}\left(\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa|=i-1,||^{\prime}\kappa||+j=\ell n}\binom{i-1}{{}^{\prime}\kappa}{\bf{}^{\prime}b}^{{}^{\prime}\kappa}\right).

Then, in characteristic 0, the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\partial_{b_{1}}T_{12}(a)&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-1}}T_{12}(a)\\
\partial_{b_{1}}T_{1i}(a)&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-1}}T_{1i}(a)\end{array}\right)

is singular if and only if the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T_{1,12}(a)&\cdots&T_{n-1,12}(a)\\
T_{1,1i}(a)&\cdots&T_{n-1,1i}(a)\end{array}\right)

is singular. Because this matrix is defined over
\mathbb{Z}[a]
, to show that it is generically nonsingular in characteristic 0, we can reduce mod
p
and find some
a\in\overline{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
for which it is nonsingular.

Let
\overline{T_{j,12}}(a)
and
\overline{T_{j,1i}}(a)
denote the reduction of the above forms mod
p
.

Recall that Legendre’s formula implies that a prime
p
divides all the multinomial coefficients
\{\binom{\ell}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}}~|~k_{j}<\ell\text{ for all }j\}
if and only if
\ell=p^{r}
. Therefore, reducing the forms
T_{j,1i}(a)
mod
p
, and using
i-1=p^{r}
, Legendre’s formula implies that .

\displaystyle\overline{T_{j,1i}}(a)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-1)^{\ell}a^{\ell}\left(\sum_{1\leq\nu\leq n-1,p^{r}\nu+j=\ell n}b_{\nu}^{p^{r}}\right)

(2.8)

(n.b. as we remark just below, only one term in the above sum is nonzero). Now, because
p\nmid n
,
p^{r}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Therefore, multiplication by
p^{-r}
determines a permutation of
\{1,\ldots,n-1\}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}
, which we denote by

\nu(j):=p^{-r}\cdot j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}=\{1,\ldots,n-1\}.

In this notation, we have

\displaystyle\overline{T_{j,12}}(a)

\displaystyle=ab_{-j}

\displaystyle\overline{T_{j,1i}}(a)

\displaystyle=(-a)^{\frac{p^{r}\nu(-j)+j}{n}}b_{\nu(-j)}^{p^{r}}

where
\pm j
and
\nu(\pm j)
denote the corresponding elements of
\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
. Now, multiplication by
p^{-r}
on
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}-\{0\}
generates a cyclic group, and so a partition of
\{1,\ldots,n-1\}
into
m
orbits
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
of size
s_{\alpha}
. Let
j_{\alpha}
denote the least element of the orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
. For ease of notation, denote

\epsilon_{\alpha}(t):=\frac{p^{r}\nu^{t}(j_{\alpha})+n-\nu^{t-1}(j_{\alpha})}{n}.

Reorder the columns of the matrix we are considering so that it is of the form

\displaystyle M:=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}M_{1}&\cdots&M_{m}\end{array}\right)

where each
M_{\alpha}
denotes the
2\times s_{\alpha}
matrix

\displaystyle M_{\alpha}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}ab_{j_{\alpha}}&ab_{\nu(j_{\alpha})}&\cdots&ab_{\nu^{s_{\alpha}-1}(j_{\alpha})}\\
(-a)^{\epsilon_{\alpha}(1)}b_{\nu(j_{\alpha})}^{p^{r}}&(-a)^{\epsilon_{\alpha}(2)}b_{\nu^{2}(j_{\alpha})}^{p^{r}}&\cdots&(-a)^{\epsilon_{\alpha}(s_{\alpha})}b_{j_{\alpha}}^{p^{r}}\end{array}\right).

Note that, by construction, for each
j
, all monomials containing
b_{j}
appear in precisely one
M_{\alpha}
.

Now the matrix (
2
) is singular at
\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{P}^{n-2}
and
a\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
if and only if its two rows are linearly dependent. Equivalently, there exists
\lambda\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
such that for all
\alpha
and
0\leq t\leq s_{\alpha}-1

ab_{\nu^{t}(j_{\alpha})}=\lambda(-a)^{\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)}b_{\nu^{t+1}(j_{\alpha})}^{p^{r}}.

(2.10)

Restrict to
a\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
. Then, by induction on
t
, we obtain that for all
j\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}

b_{j}=(-\lambda)^{\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}}(-a)^{\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}b_{j}^{p^{s_{\alpha}r}}.

Therefore, for any
b_{j}\neq 0
for
j\in\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}
(and such a
j
and
\alpha
must exist since
{\bf b}\in\mathbb{P}^{n-2}
), we have

\displaystyle b_{j}^{p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1}

\displaystyle=(-\lambda)^{-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}}(-a)^{-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}

\displaystyle=:c_{\alpha}(a)

But, if
j=\nu^{t}(j_{\alpha})
, then by Equation (
2.10
),

c_{\alpha}(a)=b_{j}^{p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1}=(-\lambda(-a)^{\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1})^{p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1}c_{\alpha}(a)^{p^{r}}.

Expanding the definition of
c_{\alpha}(a)
in terms of
\lambda
and
a
, we obtain

\displaystyle(-\lambda)^{-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}}

\displaystyle(-a)^{-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}

\displaystyle=(-\lambda)^{p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{tr}}(-a)^{(p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{tr}(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}

\displaystyle=(-\lambda)^{-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}}(-a)^{(p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{tr}(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}.

Therefore, for all
0\leq t\leq s_{\alpha}

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=(-a)^{(p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(p^{r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}

In particular,

a^{2(p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(p^{r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)}=1.

(2.11)

But,
s_{\alpha},\epsilon_{\alpha}(t),p,r\in\mathbb{N}
are fixed once and for all by our choice of
p
and
n
. In particular, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that

N>\max_{\alpha}|2(p^{s_{\alpha}r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t+1)-1)-\sum_{t=1}^{s_{\alpha}}p^{(t-1)r}(p^{r}-1)(\epsilon_{\alpha}(t)-1)|.

But, then for any primitive
N^{th}
root of unity
a\in\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, Equation
2.11
is never satisfied. Therefore, the matrix
M(a)=\left(M_{1}(a)\cdots M_{m}(a)\right)
of (
2
) has full rank for all
{\bf{}^{\prime}b}\in\mathbb{P}^{n-2}
as claimed.

Case 2:
p\mid n
.

This case is similar. We specialize to the pencil
x^{n}+ax=0
, i.e.
{\bf a}=(0,\ldots,0,a,0)
. It suffices to show there exists
a
such that
T_{12i}^{\prime}(a):=T_{12i}^{\prime}(0,\ldots,a,0)
is smooth.

As noted in the proof of Lemma
2.7
, over
\mathbb{Z}[1/(n-1)]
, and
a\neq 0
, we can use the coordinates

[b_{1}:\cdots:b_{n-2}]

on
T_{1}^{\prime}(a)
. We follow Notation
2.2
. In these coordinates and this notation, the partial derivatives of
T_{12}^{\prime}(a)
are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T_{12}(a)

\displaystyle=-2(n-1)ab_{n-1-j}

(as noted in the proof of Lemma
2.7
). Similarly, we have

\displaystyle T_{1i}^{\prime}(a)=

\displaystyle(n-1)\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-a)^{\ell}\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}|=i,||^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}||=\ell(n-1)}\binom{i}{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}~^{\prime}\mathbf{b}^{\prime(^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle\partial_{b_{j}}T_{1i}^{\prime}(a)=

\displaystyle i(n-1)\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-a)^{\ell}\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}|=i-1,||^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}||+j=\ell(n-1)}\binom{i-1}{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}~^{\prime}\mathbf{b}^{\prime(^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime})}\right)

Define

\displaystyle T_{j,12}^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle:=ab_{n-1-j}

\displaystyle T_{j,1i}^{\prime}(a)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-a)^{\ell}\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}|=i-1,||^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}||+j=\ell(n-1)}\binom{i-1}{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}~^{\prime}\mathbf{b}^{\prime(^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime})}

Just as in Case 1, the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\partial_{b_{1}}T_{12}(a)&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-2}}T_{12}(a)\\
\partial_{b_{1}}T_{1i}(a)&\cdots&\partial_{b_{n-2}}T_{1i}(a)\end{array}\right)

is everywhere nonsingular in characteristic 0 for some
a
if and only if the matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T_{1,12}^{\prime}(a)&\cdots&T_{n-2,12}^{\prime}(a)\\
T_{1,1i}^{\prime}(a)&\cdots&T_{n-2,1i}^{\prime}(a)\end{array}\right)

is everywhere nonsingular for some
a
. We now reduce this matrix mod
p
. Because
i=p^{r}+1
, the mod
p
reduction of
T_{j,1i}^{\prime}(a)
is given by

\displaystyle\overline{T_{j,1i}^{\prime}}(a)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{i-1}(-a)^{\ell}\sum_{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}~s.t.~|^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}|=i-1,||^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}||+j=\ell(n-1)}\binom{i-1}{{}^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime}}~^{\prime}\mathbf{b}^{\prime(^{\prime}\kappa^{\prime})}

In particular, because
i-1=p^{r}
, and
p^{r}\in(\mathbb{Z}/(n-1)\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
, the same arguments as above allow us to define a permutation
\nu\circlearrowleft\{1,\ldots,n-2\}=(\mathbb{Z}/(n-1)\mathbb{Z})-\{0\}
by

\nu(j)=p^{-r}j\in(\mathbb{Z}/(n-1)\mathbb{Z})-\{0\}=\{1,\ldots,n-2\}.

Using
\nu
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{T_{j,1i}^{\prime}}(a)=(-a)^{\frac{p^{r}\nu(j)+j}{n-1}}b_{\nu(j)}^{p^{r}}.

Mutatis mutandis
, we now complete the argument by the same reasoning as for Case 1.
∎

Remark 2.16
.

A similar argument shows that the Tschirnhaus hypersurface
T_{i}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
itself is generically smooth for
i=p^{r}+1
and
r\geq 0
. More generally, we see no reason not to expect this, as well as Theorem
2.12
, to hold without restriction on
i<n
. In principle, this comes down to checking whether an appropriate discriminant identically vanishes on
T_{i}
(resp.
T_{12i}
), i.e. checking a polynomial condition on the form defining
T_{i}
. However, this discriminant is a polynomial of degree
(n-1)(d-1)^{n-1}
in the coefficients of the form, and the number of terms in this polynomial grows so quickly as to make direct computation impossible except for very small
d
and
n
.

3
Algebraic Functions and Tschirnhaus Transformations

In this section, we recall the theory of Tschirnhaus transformations of algebraic functions and relate this to the Tschirnhaus complete intersections studied above.

Let
X
be an irreducible
K
-variety. We write
K(X)
for the rational functions on
X
. More generally, for a (not necessarily reducible)
K
-variety
Y
with irreducible components
\{Y_{i}\}
, let
K(Y):=\prod_{i}K(Y_{i})
.

Recall that an
algebraic function

\Phi
on
X
is a finite rational correspondence
X\dashrightarrow^{1:n}\mathbb{A}^{1}
, i.e.
\Phi
is given by a span

\textstyle{E_{\Phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{z}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
\textstyle{X}

where
\pi
is a dominant, quasi-finite map and
z
is a regular function. We say
\Phi
is
irreducible
if
E_{\Phi}
is an irreducible
K
-variety and
z
is a primitive element of the finite field extension
K(E_{\Phi})/K(X)
. As a bridge to the classical literature, we will also denote
K(E_{\Phi})
as
K(X)(\Phi)
to emphasize that
K(E_{\Phi})
is obtained from the field
K(X)
by adjoining the values of
\Phi
.

Let
\Mon(\Phi)
denote the
monodromy group
of
\Phi
, equivalently the Galois group of the normal closure of
K(X)(\Phi)/K(X)
. Let

m_{\Phi}(z):=z^{n}+a_{1}z^{n-1}+\ldots+a_{n}

denote the minimal polynomial of
z
, where the
a_{i}\in K(X)
(i.e.
m_{\Phi}(z)
is the monic generator of the ideal of
K(X)[z]
corresponding to the extension
K(X)(\Phi)
). A classical perspective describes
\Phi
as the assignment

x\mapsto\{z\in\bar{K}~|~m_{\Phi(x)}(z)=z^{n}+a_{1}(x)z^{n-1}+\ldots+a_{n}(x)=0\}.

(3.1)

For any field extension
K(X)\hookrightarrow L
, write

L(\Phi):=L\otimes_{K(X)}K(X)(\Phi).

Note that since
\{1,z,\ldots,z^{n-1}\}
is a basis for
K(X)(\Phi)
over
K(X)
, it is also a basis for
L(\Phi)
over
L
. Given this, for each
w\in L(\Phi)
, there exist unique
b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1}\in L
such that

w=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}z^{i}.

Moreover,
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}=(b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1})\in L^{n}
determines an
L
-linear transformation

T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}:L(\Phi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>L(\Phi)

given by (extending
L
-linearly) the assignment
T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}(z^{j}):=w^{j}
for each
0\leq j\leq n-1
. Note that
T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}
is an automorphism if and only if
w
is a primitive element of the extension
L(\Phi)/L
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
X
be an irreducible
K
-variety. Let
\Phi
be an irreducible algebraic function on
X
with primitive element
z\in K(X)(\Phi)
. A
Tschirnhaus transformation

T
of
\Phi
is a
\overline{K(X)}
-linear automorphism

T\colon\overline{K(X)}(\Phi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\overline{K(X)}(\Phi).

of the form

z^{j}\mapsto w^{j}=\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}z^{i}\right)^{j}

for
b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1}\in\overline{K(X)}
. We say the transformation is
rational
over
X
if
b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1}\in K(X)
. More generally, we say it is
rational
over
L/K(X)
if all
b_{i}\in L
.

Picking an integral model
Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
for
K(X)(\tilde{\mathbf{b}})/K(X)
, (i.e. a map of
K
-varieties
Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
and an isomorphism
K(Y)\cong K(X)(\tilde{\mathbf{b}})
as extensions of
K(X)
), we denote by
T(\Phi)
the algebraic function on
Y
determined by the primitive element
w\in K(Y)(\Phi)
.

Now let
\Phi
be an algebraic function as above, and
T
a Tschirnhaus transformation of
\Phi
. Let
w=T(z)
, and let the minimal polynomial of multiplication by
w
on
\overline{K(X)}(\Phi)
be given by

m_{T(\Phi)}(w):=w^{n}+c_{1}w^{n-1}+\ldots c_{n}

where
c_{i}\in L=K(Y)
. The algebraic function
T(\Phi)
on
Y
is given by the assignment

y\mapsto\{z\in\overline{K(X)}~|~m_{T(\Phi)(y)}(z)=z^{n}+c_{1}(y)z^{n-1}+\ldots+c_{n}(y)=0\}.

Recall that
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}:=X\times_{\Spec(K)}\mathbb{A}^{n}_{K}
, viewed as a variety over
X
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
X
be irreducible, and let
\Phi
be an irreducible, generically
n
-valued algebraic function on
X
. Then there is an open subvariety

\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X},

such that for all finite extensions
L/K(X)
,
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(L)
is the set of Tschirnhaus transformations of
\Phi
which are rational over
L
. In particular, the map

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

is smooth. Equivalently the parameter space of Tschirnhaus transformations
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
is smooth over
X
.

Proof.

We begin by constructing the variety
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}
. Denote the set of
\overline{K(X)}
-rational Tschirnhaus transformations of
\Phi
by
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(\overline{K(X)})
. We will show that this embeds as an explicit Zariski open subset of
\overline{K(X)}^{n}=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}(\overline{K(X)})
, and that its complement is defined over
K(X)
; we thus conclude that
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(\overline{K(X)})
is the set of geometric generic points of a variety
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
.

Let
z\in K(X)(\Phi)
be the primitive element determined by
\Phi
. Given
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}\in\overline{K(X)}^{n}
, we have a
\overline{K(X)}
-linear endomorphism

\displaystyle T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}\colon\overline{K(X)}(\Phi)

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\overline{K(X)}(\Phi)

given by

\displaystyle z^{j}

\displaystyle\mapsto\left(\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}z^{i}\right)^{j}.

Moreover, the assignment
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}\mapsto T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}
defines a
\Gal(\overline{K(X)}/K(X))
-equivariant map

T\colon\mathbb{A}^{n}(\overline{K(X)})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\End_{\overline{K(X)}}(\overline{K(X)}(\Phi))\cong\mathbb{A}^{n^{2}}(\overline{K(X)}).

By definition,
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(\overline{K(X)})
is in bijection with the set

\{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}\in\overline{K(X)}^{n}~|~T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}\in Aut_{\overline{K(X)}}(\overline{K(X)}(\Phi))\}

i.e.

\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(\overline{K(X)})=T^{-1}(Aut_{\overline{K(X)}}(\overline{K(X)}(\Phi))).

Since
Aut_{\overline{K(X)}}(\overline{K(X)}(\Phi))
is the pullback to
\overline{K(X)}
of an open subvariety of
\mathbb{A}^{n^{2}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
(i.e. the locus
\{\det\neq 0\})
) and
T
is defined over
K(X)
, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}(\overline{K(X)})\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}(\overline{K(X)}
is Zariski open and defined over
K(X)
as claimed. The remaining claims follow by direct inspection.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
\Phi
be an irreducible
n
-valued algebraic function on
X
such that
K(X)(\Phi)/K(X)
has no intermediate subfields. Let
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
be given coordinates
(b_{0},\ldots,b_{n-1})
as above, and let
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X,0}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
denote the
b_{0}
-axis. Then

\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}-\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X,0}.

Proof.

Because
K(X)(\Phi)/K(X)
has no intermediate subfields,
y\in K(X)(\Phi)
is a primitive element if and only if
y\notin K(X)
, i.e. if and only if
y
is of the form
y=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}z^{i}
with
b_{i}\neq 0
for some
i>0
.
∎

Example 3.4
.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
, viewed as the parameter space for monic, degree
n
polynomials (parametrized by their coefficients
\mathbf{a}:=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
). Let
P_{n}
be the general degree
n
polynomial, i.e.

m_{P_{n}}(z)=z^{n}+a_{1}z^{n-1}+\ldots+a_{n}.

Then the degree
n
extension
K(\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}})(P_{n})/K(\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}})
has no intermediate subfields, because it corresponds to the maximal subgroup
S_{n-1}\subset S_{n}=\Mon(P_{n})
. In particular, the space of Tschirnhaus transformations of the general degree
n
polynomial is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{P_{n}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}-\mathbb{A}^{1}_{X,0}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}-\mathbb{A}^{1}_{b_{0}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{a}

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathbb{A}^{1}_{b_{0}})\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}.

Now let
\Phi
be an irreducible algebraic function on
X
, and let
T
be a Tschirnhaus transformation of
\Phi
as above, with minimal polynomial

m_{T(\Phi)}(y):=y^{n}+c_{1}y^{n-1}+\ldots c_{n}

Observe that the assignment

x\mapsto(c_{1}(x),\ldots,c_{n}(x))

determines a rational map

X\dashrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}

which fits into a pullback square

\textstyle{E_{\Phi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{E_{P_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{n}}

In particular, the Tschirnhaus transformation
T
transforms
\Phi
into a function of
d=\dim(\Image(X\dashrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}))
variables.

We now study loci of interest in the space of Tschirnhaus transformations. The basic observation (essentially going back to Tschirnhaus
[
Ts1683
]
) is as follows. First, the collection of
n
-valued algebraic functions on
X
is given by
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
, where
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
corresponds to the function
\Phi_{\mathbf{a}}
of (
3.1
), i.e. the function

x\mapsto\{z\in\bar{K}~|~m_{\Phi_{\mathbf{a}}(x)}(z)=z^{n}+a_{1}(x)z^{n-1}+\ldots+a_{n}(x)=0\}.

Next, the assignment
(\Phi_{\mathbf{a}},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})\mapsto T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}(\Phi_{\mathbf{a}})
determines an “evaluation” map

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}

\displaystyle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.46205pt\raise 5.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ev}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.39319pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.78638pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\mathbf{a}}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a},\tilde{\mathbf{b}})

\displaystyle\mapsto T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}(\mathbf{a})

(where we write
(-)_{\mathbf{a}}
and
(-)_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}
to distinguish the different roles of the
\mathbf{a}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}
coordinates). The coordinates of
T_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}(\mathbf{a})
can be computed explicitly as follows. By definition,
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X}
corresponds to the assignment

z\mapsto\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_{i}z^{i}=y

for
z
a value of
\Phi_{\mathbf{a}}
. Passing to a Galois closure of
K(X)(\Phi)
, the transformation
T
maps the roots
z_{i}
of
m_{\Phi}
to
y_{i}
given by

y_{i}=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}b_{j}z_{i}^{j}.

In particular, the polynomial
m_{T(\Phi)}
is given by

m_{T(\Phi)}(y)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}(y-y_{i}).

i.e. the coordinates of
T_{\Phi}
are obtained (up to sign) by expanding the elementary symmetric polynomials in the
y_{i}
as polynomials in
\mathbf{b}
with coefficients given by polynomials in the coordinates
\mathbf{a}
. In particular, the
j^{th}
coefficient is a homogeneous polynomial of total degree
j
in the coordinates
\tilde{\mathbf{b}}
.

As a result, every Zariski closed subvariety
Z\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\mathbf{a}}
determines a Zariski closed subvariety

\ev^{-1}(Z)\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\tilde{\mathbf{b}}},

Specializing to a particular algebraic function
\Phi
, and its space of Tschirnhaus transformations
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}
, we obtain a Zariski closed subvariety (concretely
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}\cap\ev^{-1}(Z)
), which, by abuse of notation, we denote again by

\ev^{-1}(Z)\subset\mathcal{T}_{\Phi}.

By construction, this subvariety parametrizes Tschirnhaus transformations of
\Phi
such that
T(\Phi)
(or more precisely, the coefficients of its minimal polynomial) lie in
Z\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}_{X,\mathbf{a}}
.

We can now make contact with the Tschirnhaus complete intersections introduced in Section
2
. For
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}
, define

Z_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}:=\{\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}~|~p_{i_{1}}(\mathbf{a})=\cdots=p_{i_{k}}(\mathbf{a})=0\}

where the
p_{i}
s are as in Section
2
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
n>0
. For
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}
, define the
affine Tschirnhaus complete intersection

\tilde{T}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})
to be

\tilde{T}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n}):=\ev^{-1}(Z_{i_{1}\cdots i_{j}})\subset\mathcal{T}_{P_{n}}\subset(\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\tilde{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathbb{A}^{1}_{b_{0}=0})\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}.

Projecting onto
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
gives the family
\tilde{T}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
.

Similarly, define the
Tschirnhaus complete intersection

T_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})\subset(\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}-\{[1:0:\cdots:0]\})\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

to be the (fiberwise) projectivization of the family
\tilde{T}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
.

Define the
reduced affine Tschirnhaus complete intersection
by

\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n}):=T_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\cap\{b_{0}=0\}.

Similarly, define the
reduced Tschirnhaus complete intersection

T^{\prime}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})\subset\mathbb{P}^{n-2}_{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}

to be the (fiberwise) projectivization of the family
\tilde{T}^{\prime}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}
.

Lemma
2.9
can now be equivalently restated as follows.

Lemma 3.6
.

For all
n
and all
1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})=T^{n}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}

as subvarieties of
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-1}_{\mathbf{b}}
, where the right hand side denotes the Tschirnhaus complete intersection of Definition
2.5
.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle T^{\prime}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(P_{n})=T^{n^{\prime}}_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}

as subvarieties of
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{\mathbf{a}}\times\mathbb{P}^{n-2}_{{}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}}
.

4
The Resolvent Degree of a Dominant Map

Recall the following (see
[
Br75
,
AS76
,
FW19
]
).

Definition 4.1

(
Resolvent degree).

Let
Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
be a generically finite dominant map of

-varieties. Its
resolvent degree

\RD(Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X)
is the minimum
d
for which there exists a dense Zariski open

and a tower of generically finite dominant maps

such that

factors through a dominant map

and such that for each
